Runtime Docs Audit

Contents

  • Code defects the docs items depend on
  • 1. Bun guide
  • DOC-B1 The guide states the instrumentation model backwards and never names the plugin
  • DOC-B2 The Bun integrations table lists three integrations that are not installed and omits eleven that are
  • DOC-B3 Express, Koa, Hapi, Fastify and Next.js work on Bun and no page says so; Hono has an undocumented bundling caveat
  • DOC-B4 Guide hygiene: no Bun version requirement, `npm install` in the Hono guide, no breadcrumbs page
  • 2. Deno guide
  • DOC-D1 The guide never mentions the runtime hook or which instrumentation is automatic
  • DOC-D2 The Deno integrations table lists 8 auto-enabled integrations; the SDK installs 39
  • DOC-D3 The permission section is incomplete and the two commands are shown as alternatives
  • DOC-D4 Fastify and Hono work on Deno without the hook, Express, Koa and Hapi need it and crash with it, and no page says any of this
  • DOC-D5 The Deno v7-to-v8 migration include files are copies of the Bun ones
  • DOC-D6 The guide shows only the `npm:` specifier, never `deno add` or an import map, and events name a registry that does not ship the package
  • 3. Cloudflare guide
  • DOC-C1 Four places document the `@sentry/cloudflare/nodejs_compat` entrypoint that v11 removes
  • DOC-C2 `features/d1.mdx` documents a removed function and hides that D1 is automatic
  • DOC-C3 `enableRpcTracePropagation` appears in three places and has no options entry; v11 replaces it
  • DOC-C4 The Vite plugin page documents `_experimental` options that no longer exist and says the plugin is a no-op by default
  • DOC-C5 `wrapRequestHandler` is imported from the main entry, and the `honoIntegration` page survives its removal
  • DOC-C6 No page lists which bindings produce spans, and the list changed twice this year
  • DOC-C7 The frameworks section is missing React Router, says "Pages", and the TanStack Start page cannot render its wrangler step
  • DOC-C8 Snippet hygiene across the Cloudflare includes
  • 4. Cross-cutting
  • X1 "Tracing is enabled by default" on all three runtimes contradicts the guides
  • X2 There is no v10-to-v11 page, and `MIGRATION.md` does not say how to enable channel instrumentation per runtime
  • X3 There is no runtime-by-framework or runtime-by-integration matrix, and every table is hand-maintained

  • DOC-R1 Stream-mode includes call `'static'` the default; develop defaults to `'stream'`

  • DOC-R14 waitUntil() section covers spans only; errors inside ctx.waitUntil() are never captured

  • DOC-R20 Bun OpenTelemetry setup includes import @sentry/node-core and call APIs that no longer exist

  • Checked, not a defect
  • N1 Bun outgoing `node:http` and `fetch` requests are instrumented and propagate
  • N2 Importing `@sentry/deno` before the library does not block injection
  • N3 `compatibility_date: 2024-09-23` with `nodejs_compat` boots the SDK
  • N4 The SvelteKit and Remix pages under `frameworks/` do carry Cloudflare content
  • Suggested order

Runtime docs audit: Bun, Deno, Cloudflare

What the Bun, Deno and Cloudflare guides in getsentry/sentry-docs say, checked claim by claim against the shipped @sentry/bun, @sentry/deno and @sentry/cloudflare packages, with @sentry/node as the baseline. It also answers which frameworks work on each runtime and whether the docs say so. This is the DOC7 item the prior audit (https://gist.github.com/isaacs/2560b72b22a7007bc8098bf33c83020d) left open; its items are cited as gist DOC1 and so on and not repeated unless a repro changed their state.

SDK repo~/dev/sentry-javascript, audited at 6f2da7483d (develop, v11 line, package version 10.67.0) in a detached worktree at ~/dev/sentry-worktrees/audit-runtime-docs
Docs repo~/dev/sentry-docs at 046ce5942 (2026-09-01)
Published@sentry/{bun,deno,cloudflare,node} 10.73.0 on npm. The docs describe v10; develop already carries the v11 breaking changes, and MIGRATION.md lists them.
Runtimesnode v26.5.0, bun 1.4.0, deno 2.9.0 and 2.8.3 (the CI pin), wrangler 4.56.0 to 4.129.0
Datesections 1 to 4 on 2026-09-02 with ticket states read the same day, section 5 on 2026-09-06 and 2026-09-07. A batch of audit issues from the gist author landed in sentry-javascript that day (#23881 to #23899).

Nothing was committed. Every behavior claim was run against the built packages; the probes are in probes/, their outputs in probes/results/, and probes/rerun.sh plus probes/FW-run.sh re-run all of them. The proposed quick starts in quickstart/ were built as fresh apps in qs/ and run on the develop build and on the published 10.73.0; quickstart/VERIFICATION.md maps every guide claim to a result. The main checkout was fast-forwarded twice during the audit, so the packages were built in the pinned worktree.

[proven] a probe confirms the claim. [read] the claim rests on reading (docs wording, CI config). P0 a page that loses data for a common setup. P1 a page that is wrong or missing for something users will hit. P2 consistency and hygiene.

Coverage of the 21 items in sections 1 to 4, plus the 4 checked items:

Issue proposals

Every item below carries a proposed GitHub issue for getsentry/sentry-docs, a title and a description. The proposals describe only wiring and behavior verified to work on the develop line today, name the page to change, and state verified limits as plain facts. They ask for no SDK changes and contain no workarounds that rely on internal paths or hand-written copies of SDK code.

Snapshot check on 2026-09-07

Docs master moved to b568ebbd3 (2026-09-04) after the audit snapshot 046ce5942; none of the 70 commits touches the Bun, Deno, Cloudflare, Hono or Elysia guides or their platform includes, so every file and line citation below still holds. The SDK develop branch moved to d6426ad581 (2026-09-07); the only behavior change in the audited packages is the Deno stream-cancellation fix c619da370, which affects report finding F24 and no docs item here.

Code defects the docs items depend on

These were found while checking the pages. They are not docs work, but several pages cannot be written correctly until they are decided. One line each; the probe outputs are referenced from the docs items.

Code itemWhat it isBlocksTicket
K1bun run installs 22 channel integrations that never fire; the only warning is debug-level (packages/server-utils/src/orchestrion/detect.ts:37-43)DOC-B1#23882 (open)
K2Deno fetch gets no span and no sentry-trace/baggage (probes/results/PROP-deno.json)DOC-D2#23891 (open)
K3Any node:module load hook on Deno breaks require('*.json'), so @sentry/deno/import crashes Express, Hapi, Fastify and mysql2 at import (probes/results/D-JSON.txt, FW-deno-hook.txt). Deno bug; a second load hook that sets format: 'json' for .json URLs works around it (deep probe X03), so an SDK-side shim in register.ts is feasibleDOC-D1, DOC-D4denoland/deno#36240 (open), fix denoland/deno#36243 (open); needs a sentry-javascript ticket
K4@sentry/deno init() never warns when the hook is absent (probes/results/INIT-deno.json)DOC-D1#23892 (open)
K5packages/deno/README.md:70 and src/import.mjs:10 show --import=@sentry/deno/import, which Deno resolves as a file path and rejects (probes/results/D-HOOK.txt)DOC-D1none, needs one
K6packages/cloudflare/README.md:82-99 imports wrapRequestHandler from the main entry, which v11 no longer exports (probes/results/CF-EXPORTS.txt)DOC-C5none, needs one
K7Hono on Bun loses route names and error capture when hono is inlined by Bun.build; keeping hono external restores both (probes/FW-hono-bun.mjs)DOC-B3none, needs one
K8Events from @sentry/deno report sdk.packages[0].name = "denoland:sentry" (packages/deno/src/client.ts:36) while the package ships on npm onlyDOC-D6none, needs one
K9denoCronIntegration JSDoc says "Enabled by default" (packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-cron.ts:63); it is not in the defaultsDOC-D2none, needs one
K10Commit 6f2da7483d, on the branch jp/remove-rate-limiter that develop was fast-forwarded to during the audit, disables rate-limiter spans and has no MIGRATION.md entryDOC-C6not pushed yet
K11Bun plugin build: an instrumented module that first evaluates inside a request (mysql's lazily required lib/Connection.js, or a dynamic import() of a driver in a handler) never gets spans, on develop and 10.73.0, bun 1.4.0 and 1.3.14. Root cause, found by the deep probe (X02): Bun's node:diagnostics_channel garbage-collects a channel and its subscribers when nothing holds a strong reference, and the SDK drops the channel object it subscribes to (packages/server-utils/src/tracing-channel.ts:200-208). Fix: retain the channels. Workarounds: import drivers and create pools at module scope, or import "mysql/lib/Connection"DOC-B1, DOC-B3none, needs one (an SDK fix, plus a Bun report)
K12@sentry/deno/import only transforms packages under a local node_modules; from Deno's npm cache nothing is injected and nothing warns (qs/qs10/deno-cache)DOC-D1none, needs one

1. Bun guide

Pages: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx and guides/bun/configuration/integrations/contextlines.mdx. No config.yml, no features/, no frameworks/, no breadcrumbs page (the common page lists javascript.bun in notSupported and there is no guide override, unlike Deno).

DOC-B1The guide states the instrumentation model backwards and never names the pluginP0proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Rewrite the Bun guide's instrumentation step around @sentry/bun/plugin

Description

The Bun guide at guides/bun/index.mdx:94 and :106 tells the user to run bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js, and the alert at :96-100 states that Sentry's auto-instrumentation does not work with bundled code and that bundling requires manual instrumentation. @sentry/bun/plugin is not named anywhere on the page, and logs is used as an onboarding block at :182 but is missing from the options at :20-22.

What was observed on bun 1.4.0: a bundle built with Bun.build({ plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin records library and framework spans, while running the same app unbundled with bun run records none. packages/bun/src/plugin.ts:14 states the plugin is build-only. The unbundled diagnostic message is printed only with debug: true.

What to change:

  • Keep the --preload step, described as what guarantees init order.
  • Add a "Build with the Sentry plugin" step showing Bun.build({ entrypoints: [...], plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) imported from @sentry/bun/plugin.
  • Replace the alert at :96-100 with a plain statement: library and framework instrumentation requires a build with the plugin; bun run on unbundled source records manual spans plus Bun.serve, node:http and fetch spans.
  • Add logs to the onboarding options at :20-22, or remove the block at :182.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-B1

gist DOC1, re-run. guides/bun/index.mdx:96-100:

Sentry's auto-instrumentation does not work with bundled code, including Bun's single-file executables. [...] If you need to bundle your application, you'll need to manually instrument your code.

and :94, :106 tell the user to run bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js. On this commit the opposite holds: only a bundle built with @sentry/bun/plugin records library and framework spans, and bun run records none (K1). packages/bun/src/plugin.ts:14: "This is BUILD-ONLY. Runtime instrumentation (bun run) is currently not supported." The plugin is not mentioned anywhere in the guide. logs is referenced as an onboarding block at :182 but not offered at :20-22.

A user who follows the page gets 42 installed integrations, no database, AI or framework spans, and no visible message.

Repro probes/B-PLUGIN-build.mjs + probes/B-PLUGIN-app.mjs, bun 1.4.0, verdict: confirmed. probes/results/B-PLUGIN.txt:

build ok: true []
-- bundled output run:   [Sentry] Bundler plugin ran, injected libraries=["express"]   {"orchestrion":{"bundler":["express"]}}
-- same app unbundled:   [Sentry] No diagnostics-channel injection detected. ...       {"orchestrion":{}}

The unbundled warning only prints with debug: true (probes/results/INIT-bun.json).

Test coverage the bun-mysql e2e app (dev-packages/e2e-tests/test-applications/bun-mysql/build.ts:6-21) pins the plugin path. Nothing pins the --preload path the guide recommends.

Work item. Rewrite "Apply Instrumentation to Your App": keep --preload for init order, add a "Build with the Sentry plugin" step with Bun.build({ plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }), and replace the alert with a plain statement that bun run on unbundled source records manual spans plus the Bun.serve, node:http and fetch spans (see DOC-B3 for what that means per framework). Add logs to the onboarding buttons or remove the block. Land with K1 so the page and the warning agree.

Prior art _(related history only)_. sentry-docs#15539 (closed 2025-11) put the --preload advice on the page via sentry-docs#15575, before the plugin existed. sentry-docs#16843 (merged 2026-03) reframed the AI pages as "manual on Bun" without touching the Bun guide. No issue names the bundling paragraph or @sentry/bun/plugin. #14202 (open since 2024-11) is a user asking exactly this question.

DOC-B2The Bun integrations table lists three integrations that are not installed and omits eleven that areP1proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Correct the Bun integrations table in platform-includes/.../javascript.bun.mdx

Description

platform-includes/configuration/integrations/javascript.bun.mdx:5-37 lists dedupeIntegration (:9), nativeNodeFetchIntegration (:24) and inboundFiltersIntegration (:14) as auto-enabled, and pinoIntegration at :47, while fsIntegration is shown for Bun with no row.

What was observed: the Bun SDK installs 42 integrations. It ships fetchIntegration rather than nativeNodeFetchIntegration, installs inbound filtering as EventFilters, and does not install dedupeIntegration. Integrations installed with no row in the table: fetchIntegration, bunHttpServerIntegration, processSessionIntegration, expressIntegration, fastifyIntegration, hapiIntegration, koaIntegration, awsIntegration, langGraphIntegration, postgresJsIntegration, spanStreamingIntegration. The list is produced by getDefaultIntegrations() in packages/bun/src/sdk.ts:47-79 and packages/server-utils/src/integrations/index.ts:30-62. The channel-based integrations only produce spans after a Bun.build with @sentry/bun/plugin.

What to change:

  • Drop the dedupeIntegration row; rename nativeNodeFetchIntegration to fetchIntegration; rename inboundFiltersIntegration to the installed eventFiltersIntegration.
  • Add rows for the eleven installed integrations listed above.
  • Add a "Requires @sentry/bun/plugin" column and tick it for the channel-based rows.
  • Fix the gating so common/configuration/integrations/pino.mdx is shown for Bun, and give fsIntegration a row or hide it.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-B2

platform-includes/configuration/integrations/javascript.bun.mdx:5-37 against probes/results/INIT-bun.json (42 installed):

Table says auto-enabled, SDK does not installLine
dedupeIntegration:9
nativeNodeFetchIntegration (Bun ships fetchIntegration):24
inboundFiltersIntegration (installed as EventFilters):14
SDK installs, table has no row
fetchIntegration, bunHttpServerIntegration, processSessionIntegration
expressIntegration, fastifyIntegration, hapiIntegration, koaIntegration
awsIntegration, langGraphIntegration, postgresJsIntegration, spanStreamingIntegration

pinoIntegration sits in the table (:47) while common/configuration/integrations/pino.mdx is not shown for Bun; fsIntegration is shown for Bun and has no row. None of the 22 channel rows says the span only appears after a Bun.build with the plugin.

Test coverage none for docs tables. The generating source is packages/bun/src/sdk.ts:47-79 and packages/server-utils/src/integrations/index.ts:30-62.

Work item. Regenerate the table from getDefaultIntegrations(), add a "Requires @sentry/bun/plugin" column, fix the pino and fs gating. See X3 for generating it.

Prior art _(nothing found)_. #23887 (open) is about the SDK defaults diverging from Node, not the table.

DOC-B3Express, Koa, Hapi, Fastify and Next.js work on Bun and no page says so; Hono has an undocumented bundling caveatP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a frameworks page to the Bun guide and a Bun caveat to the Hono guide

Description

The Bun guide has no frameworks section, and guides/express/, guides/fastify/, guides/koa/, guides/hapi/ and guides/nextjs/ contain no mention of Bun. guides/hono/index.mdx:12,84-85,290-292 covers Bun; guides/elysia/index.mdx:5 inherits from Bun.

Observed on bun 1.4.0: under bun run, Express, Koa, Hapi and Fastify produce URL-named server transactions (GET /user/42, source url), no child spans and no captured error. After Bun.build with sentryBunPlugin, the same apps produce route-named transactions (GET /user/:id, GET /user/{id} on Hapi), middleware/router/handler spans and captured errors (auto.http.express, auto.middleware.koa, auto.function.hapi, auto.function.fastify), matching Node. Hono via @sentry/hono/bun works with bun run and after bundling only while hono stays external; with hono inlined, transactions fall back to GET /user/42 and no error is captured. The per-framework Bun items DOC-F1 to DOC-F17 carry the verified wiring for every other framework.

What to change:

  • Add guides/bun/frameworks/index.mdx with a table: framework, behavior under bun run, behavior after Bun.build + plugin, guide to follow.
  • Note that database clients must be created at module scope.
  • Add a "Bun" pointer to the Express, Fastify, Koa and Hapi guides.
  • Add "keep hono external when bundling" to the Hono guide's Bun tab.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-B3

The Bun guide has no frameworks section, and the Express, Fastify, Koa, Hapi and Next.js guides do not mention Bun (grep of guides/{express,fastify,koa,hapi,nextjs}/ for "bun": 0 hits). The Hono guide covers Bun (guides/hono/index.mdx:12,84-85,290-292) and the Elysia guide inherits from Bun (guides/elysia/index.mdx:5). What the SDK does, from probes/FW.mjs run three ways (probes/FW-run.sh, bun 1.4.0, node v26.5.0 baseline):

Frameworkbun run (guide's setup)Bun.build + sentryBunPluginNode baseline
ExpressGET /user/42 (source url), no child spans, error not capturedGET /user/:id (source route), middleware + handler spans, error auto.http.express handled=falsesame as bundled Bun
Koasame as aboveGET /user/:id, router span, error auto.middleware.koasame
Hapisame as aboveGET /user/{id}, router span, error auto.function.hapisame
Fastifysame as aboveGET /user/:id, handler span, error auto.function.fastifysame
Hono (@sentry/hono/bun)GET /user/:id, error auto.http.hono.context_errorsame, but only if hono stays external; with hono inlined: GET /user/42, no error (K7)same
Elysia (@sentry/elysia)not run here (root @sinclair/typebox too old for the probe); covered by dev-packages/e2e-tests/test-applications/elysia-bun/tests/{errors,isolation,propagation,transactions}.test.ts
Next.js (bun --bun next start)not run here; covered by nextjs-16-bun/tests/ (9 files) using bunHttpServerIntegration({ spans: false }) + fetchIntegration() from @sentry/bun in sentry.server.config.ts:1-5

Verbatim rows are in probes/results/FW-bun-run.txt and FW-bun-plugin.txt; the Hono bundling check is probes/FW-hono-bun.mjs + FW-hono-bun-build.mjs:

hono unbundled:              transactions ["GET /user/:id (route)", "GET /error (route)"], errors [auto.http.hono.context_error]
hono bundled, hono inlined:  transactions ["GET /user/42 (url)",    "GET /error (url)"],   errors []
hono bundled, hono external: transactions ["GET /user/:id (route)", "GET /error (route)"], errors [auto.http.hono.context_error]

Test coverage dev-packages/bun-integration-tests/suites/{orchestrion-mysql,orchestrion-postgres,hono-sdk} and the e2e apps above. No Bun suite runs Express, Koa, Hapi or Fastify; no test bundles a Hono app.

Work item. Add guides/bun/frameworks/index.mdx with the table above (three columns: works with bun run, works after Bun.build + plugin, guide to follow), a note to create database clients at module scope (K11), and a "Bun" note in the four Node framework guides pointing there. Add the "keep hono external" caveat to the Hono guide's Bun tab until K7 is fixed. Add a Next.js-on-Bun section to the Next.js guide from nextjs-16-bun/sentry.server.config.ts, with the limits from #19323.

Prior art _(tracked, partial)_. #19323 (open, 2026-02) "Next.js/Bun: OTel instrumentation gaps for HTTP tracing" is the SDK side of the Next.js row. #17779 (open) is a user hitting the Express row under bun run. Nothing names a Bun frameworks page or the Hono bundling caveat.

DOC-B4Guide hygiene: no Bun version requirement, npm install in the Hono guide, no breadcrumbs pageP2read

Proposed issue docs-change

Fix Bun guide hygiene: version floor, Hono install command, breadcrumbs, v7 wording

Description

guides/bun/index.mdx:12 pulls the getting-started-prerequisites include, but no javascript.bun.mdx variant of it exists, so the page states no Bun version floor. guides/hono/index.mdx:84-85 installs the Bun SDK with npm install @sentry/bun, while the Bun guide uses bun add. common/configuration/integrations/breadcrumbs.mdx lists Bun under notSupported with no Bun-specific override. platform-includes/performance/automatic-instrumentation-intro/javascript.bun.mdx still says the SDK "provides a Tracing integration", which is v7 wording.

What was observed: CI runs the Bun SDK against Bun 1.3.14 (.github/workflows/build.yml:522), which is the version the SDK is verified on.

What to change:

  • Add platform-includes/getting-started-prerequisites/javascript.bun.mdx stating the Bun version the SDK is tested against (1.3.14).
  • Change the Bun install tab in guides/hono/index.mdx:84-85 to bun add @sentry/bun.
  • Either add a Bun breadcrumbs page or remove Bun from the notSupported list in common/configuration/integrations/breadcrumbs.mdx, matching whichever the SDK installs.
  • Delete the "provides a Tracing integration" sentence from platform-includes/performance/automatic-instrumentation-intro/javascript.bun.mdx.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-B4

  • guides/bun/index.mdx:12 pulls getting-started-prerequisites; no javascript.bun.mdx exists for it, so no Bun version floor is stated. CI pins 1.3.14 (.github/workflows/build.yml:522).

Proposed issue docs-change

Description

What was observed on deno 2.9.0, with and without a first-line import '@sentry/deno/import';: Fastify works with no hook, producing GET /user/:id (source route), a handler span and an auto.function.fastify error, because Fastify publishes its own node:diagnostics_channel events. Hono via @sentry/hono/deno works with no hook. Express, Koa and Hapi without the hook produce URL-named transactions (GET /user/42, source url), no child spans and no captured error. With the hook, all three fail at import: Express and Hapi with SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':', Koa with Cannot resolve module "./index.js". Fastify also fails at import under the hook. The per-framework Deno items DOC-F18 to DOC-F34 carry the verified wiring for every other framework.

What to change:

  • Add guides/deno/frameworks/index.mdx with a table: framework, behavior without the hook, behavior with the hook, guide to follow.
  • State plainly that Fastify and Hono are supported on Deno with no extra step, and that Express, Koa and Hapi produce URL-named server transactions without error capture, and fail to import under the hook.
  • Add a "Deno" note to the Fastify guide (works as is) and to the Express, Koa and Hapi guides (server transactions only), each linking to the new page.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-D4

The Deno guide has no frameworks section; the Fastify, Express, Koa and Hapi guides do not mention Deno (0 hits). The Hono guide covers Deno (guides/hono/index.mdx:88-89,320-322). What the SDK does (probes/FW-run.sh, deno 2.9.0; probes/results/FW-deno-nohook.txt, FW-deno-hook.txt):

Hapisame as Expresscrash at import (K3)
Koasame as Expresscrash: Cannot resolve module "./index.js" from ".../generator-function/require.mjs" (a second Deno loader problem under the hook)

DOC-F1Hono on Bun: documented partialP1proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Document Hono on Bun: unbundled run and the Bun.build plugin variant

Description

The Hono guide names Bun at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hono/index.mdx:12, has a Bun install tab at hono/index.mdx:84-86 and a Bun setup snippet at hono/index.mdx:290-317, but it never shows how to start the app or what the runtime delivers. The Bun guide teaches only bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js (bun/index.mdx:106) and carries a "Bundled Code Limitation" alert at bun/index.mdx:96-98. sentryBunPlugin, @sentry/bun/plugin and Bun.build appear nowhere in the docs.

Verified on Hono 4.10.x with @sentry/hono/bun 10.67.0 on Bun 1.4.0: install @sentry/hono plus @sentry/bun, call app.use(sentry(app, { dsn, tracesSampleRate: 1.0 })) on a fresh new Hono(), export { port, fetch: app.fetch }, and run bun app.ts. No instrument file and no --preload are needed, because the middleware calls Sentry.init itself. Building the same entry with Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./app.ts'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', sourcemap: 'external', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin and running bun dist/app.js keeps every capability.

Works today:

  • Unhandled handler errors, with the route as the transaction name
  • Parameterized route names (GET /users/:id, source route)
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 database spans without any build step
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • Drivers that do not publish node:diagnostics_channel events themselves, such as mysql@2, produce spans only in the Bun.build bundle.
  • Passing external to Bun.build splits hono from @sentry/hono and the patches stop applying.

What to change:

  • guides/hono/index.mdx: add a "Run your app" step with a Bun tab showing bun app.ts and the Bun.build + sentryBunPlugin() variant, and state that the --preload step is not needed here.
  • guides/bun/index.mdx:96-98: rewrite the alert so it describes the plugin build as the supported way to instrument bundled code.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F1, cell M-hono-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hono/index.mdx — add a "Run your app" step with a Bun tab (unbundled `bun app.ts` and the `Bun.build` + `sentryBunPlugin()` variant); and rewrite the alert at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx:96-98, which is contradicted by this run.

The Bun tab shows the `sentry(app, {...})` middleware but never shows how to start the app, and never states what works. The page must say that on Bun 1.4.0 the app runs unbundled as `bun app.ts` (the `sentry()` middleware calls `Sentry.init` itself, so the `bun --preload ./instrument.js` step the Bun guide teaches at bun/index.mdx:106 is not needed here), and that in that shape errors, route names, request isolation, incoming `sentry-trace`/`baggage` continuation, outgoing `fetch` spans, `mysql`/`mysql2` db spans and logs all work. It must also say the same app built with `Bun.build({ target: 'bun', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] })` from `@sentry/bun/plugin` and run as `bun dist/app.js` keeps every one of those capabilities — which directly contradicts the Bun guide's alert at bun/index.mdx:96-98 that "Sentry's auto-instrumentation does not work with bundled code". Neither `sentryBunPlugin`, `@sentry/bun/plugin` nor `Bun.build` appears anywhere in the docs today.

Where the docs stand: ~/dev/sentry-docs/docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hono/index.mdx:12 ("supports Hono 4+ across multiple runtimes: Cloudflare Workers, Node.js, Bun, and Deno"); hono/index.mdx:84-86 (Bun tab: `npm install @sentry/bun`); hono/index.mdx:290-317 (Bun tab of the setup snippet: `import { sentry } from "@sentry/hono/bun"`); ~/dev/sentry-docs/docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx:96-98 ("Bundled Code Limitation" alert); bun/index.mdx:106 (`bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js`)

What ran: Hono 4.10.x (`hono@4`) with `@sentry/hono/bun` 10.67.0 (develop, v11 line) on Bun 1.4.0 (`bun`), both unbundled (`bun app.ts`) and as a `Bun.build` bundle with `sentryBunPlugin`. Cell record.

DOC-F2Elysia on Bun: documented partialP1proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Add a Bun database instrumentation section to the Elysia guide

Description

The Elysia guide is already Bun-first and correct as published: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/elysia/index.mdx:4-5 declares sdk: sentry.javascript.elysia with fallbackGuide: javascript.bun, elysia/index.mdx:23-25 lists the prerequisites, and elysia/index.mdx:56-57 shows bun add @sentry/elysia. What it does not cover is how database and library instrumentation behaves on Bun, and the alert at elysia/index.mdx:47-50 ("The @sentry/elysia SDK handles all instrumentation natively") reads as an unconditional promise.

Verified on Elysia 1.4 with @sentry/elysia 10.67.0 on Bun 1.4.0: Sentry.init either inline in the entry or in an instrument.ts imported first, Sentry.withElysia(new Elysia()) wrapping the fresh instance before any route is chained, then bun app.ts. No --preload, no @elysiajs/opentelemetry, and no build step for the base capabilities.

Works today:

  • Unhandled handler errors via the SDK's global onError hook
  • Route names GET /users/:id with source route, on the success and error paths
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation, plus response trace headers
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 database spans with no build step
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • On Bun only libraries that publish node:diagnostics_channel events themselves are instrumented under a plain bun app.ts; every other instrumented library needs the app bundled with Bun.build({ target: 'bun', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin, run as bun dist/app.js.
  • Events in a handler carry no transaction name, because the name is set after the handler runs.

What to change:

  • guides/elysia/index.mdx: add a "Database and library instrumentation on Bun" section after Configure with the bundle recipe, and qualify the alert at :47-50 for Bun.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F2, cell M-elysia-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/elysia/index.mdx — new "Database and library instrumentation on Bun" section after Configure, plus a qualifier on the alert at :47-50.

The Bun-first wiring on this page is correct as published — errors, route names, isolation, incoming trace, outgoing fetch and logs all work following it. What is missing is the database/library limit observed on Bun: under a plain `bun app.ts` the SDK produces db spans only for drivers that publish `node:diagnostics_channel` events themselves (`mysql2` does, so it needs no build step); for `mysql`@2 and every other instrumented library (Express, Koa, Fastify, Hapi, AI SDKs) there are no spans at all unless the app is bundled with `Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./app.ts'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', sourcemap: 'external', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] })` from `@sentry/bun/plugin` and run as `bun dist/app.js`. The alert at :47-50 ("handles all instrumentation natively") reads as an unconditional promise and must be qualified for Bun. Separately, the shipped SDK reports `sentry.javascript.bun` on every event, transaction and span, contradicting this page's own `sdk: sentry.javascript.elysia` frontmatter — the verified finding is that this is an SDK defect to fix in code, so no docs sentence should be written for it and the frontmatter should stay as-is.

Where the docs stand: ~/dev/sentry-docs/docs/platforms/javascript/guides/elysia/index.mdx:4-5 (`sdk: sentry.javascript.elysia`, `fallbackGuide: javascript.bun`); elysia/index.mdx:23-25 (prerequisites: Elysia v1.4.0+, Bun or Node.js 18+); elysia/index.mdx:47-50 ("You do **not** need `@elysiajs/opentelemetry`. It must split database instrumentation by driver rather than stating one rule: `mysql2` 3.20.0 and later publish their own `node:diagnostics_channel` events, so `query()`, `execute()`, the promise wrapper and pool connections are all instrumented by plain `bun run` with no build step and no module-scope-connection requirement; `mysql`@2 and other drivers Sentry instruments by code injection are not. And the Bun guide's alert at bun/index.mdx:96-98 is backwards for this cell and must be corrected: bundling with `sentryBunPlugin()` is how those other libraries get instrumented on Bun, not a thing that disables instrumentation.

Proposed issue new-page

Add an Oak on Bun page covering the Bun.serve routes map

Description

Oak appears nowhere in the docs, on any runtime. Verified on Oak 17.2.0 with @sentry/bun 10.67.0 on Bun 1.4.0. Oak is JSR-only, so installation needs an .npmrc with @jsr:registry=https://npm.jsr.io and npm install "@oak/oak@npm:@jsr/oak__oak@17.2.0". Put Sentry.init in instrument.ts imported first, create the mysql2 connection at module scope, and mount the app through Bun.serve. For route names, build the routes map from the Oak router at startup and keep fetch as the fallback: const handler = req => app.fetch(req); const routes = {}; for (const r of router) routes[r.path] = handler; Bun.serve({ port, routes, fetch: handler }).

Works today:

  • Unhandled handler errors
  • Route names GET /users/:id with source route once the routes map is passed
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 database spans with no build step
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes correlated to the request trace

Known limits:

  • Mounted with fetch only, server spans are named GET with source url; routes using regex constraints, optional params or (.*) are named imprecisely unless listed explicitly in the map.
  • app.listen() is not a working mount shape with @sentry/bun.
  • Browser-side capture was not exercised.

What to change:

  • New page docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/oak.mdx with the JSR install, the Bun.serve mount and routes map, and the capability list.
  • common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx: add that server span names come from the Bun.serve routes map.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F7, cell M-oak-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/oak.mdx (or guides/oak/index.mdx with fallbackGuide: javascript.bun, the Elysia pattern). Also: common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx must gain a section stating that server span names come from the Bun.serve routes map.

A page must say: Oak 17 runs on Bun, but only mounted as Bun.serve({ fetch: req => app.fetch(req) }); app.listen() is effectively unsupported with @sentry/bun. Install needs the JSR npm registry because Oak is JSR-only: an .npmrc with @jsr:registry=https://npm.jsr.io plus npm install "@oak/oak@npm:@jsr/oak__oak@17.2.0". On Bun the server span name comes from the routes map passed to Bun.serve, so a fetch-only mount produces spans named GET with source url, in both lifecycles, bundled or not. Document the verified workaround: build a routes map from the Oak router at startup and keep fetch as the fallback. Errors, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans, mysql2 database spans and logs all work. Client-side errors were not exercised and must not be claimed.

Where the docs stand: guides/bun/index.mdx:1-214 (the whole Bun guide; names no framework, never mentions Bun.serve or a routes map); common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx:18 (only description of Bun.serve instrumentation, says nothing about span naming); guides/bun/ has no framework subtree; grep for oak and for the JSR npm registry over docs and platform-includes returns zero hits.

Verified on Next.js 15.5.4 (app router) with @sentry/nextjs 10.67.0 on Bun 1.4.0: withSentryConfig imported from the @sentry/nextjs/config subpath in next.config.mjs, the standard instrumentation.ts with the nodejs branch and captureRequestError re-exported as onRequestError, then bun --bun next build and bun --bun next start. Install @sentry/bun alongside @sentry/nextjs and add fetchIntegration() and bunHttpServerIntegration({ spans: false }) from it to integrations in sentry.server.config.ts.

Works today:

  • Unhandled route-handler errors
  • Route names GET /api/users/[id] with source route
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans and propagation headers, with fetchIntegration() added
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • Database spans are not recorded on Bun in this setup.
  • Do not name the project root, or any ancestor of your app/ directory, app: an extra app path segment degrades transaction and span names to source url.
  • Edge-runtime routes, middleware and server actions were not exercised.

  • New page docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/nextjs.mdx with the install, the @sentry/nextjs/config import, the two @sentry/bun integrations, the bun --bun build and start commands, and which instrumentation.ts branch runs under bun --bun.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F10, cell M-nextjs-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/nextjs.mdx (new), plus a "Project directory named `app`" troubleshooting note under docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nextjs/

A page must say Next.js 15.5.4 (app router) builds and serves under `bun --bun next build` / `bun --bun next start` and that `@sentry/nextjs` initializes there, then name the three things the Node path does not need. (1) Install `@sentry/bun` alongside `@sentry/nextjs` and add `fetchIntegration()` and `bunHttpServerIntegration({ spans: false })` from it to `integrations` in `sentry.server.config.ts`; without them outgoing `fetch` produces no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers, because the Node SDK's HTTP instrumentation relies on undici diagnostics channels Bun never publishes. (2) After `Sentry.init()`, keep a strong reference to the tracing channels (`mysql2:query`, `mysql2:execute`, `mysql2:connect`, `mysql2:pool:connect`, `ioredis:command`, `ioredis:connect`) on `globalThis` — Bun collects channels that only Sentry's subscriber references, so the subscriptions made at init are gone a tick later and no db span is recorded. (3) State which `instrumentation.ts` branch runs under `bun --bun`, since the documented `register()` at :144-147 covers only `nodejs` and `edge`. A separate troubleshooting note applies to Node and Bun equally: do not name the project root, or any ancestor of your `app/` directory, `app` — Next.js derives the route on its OpenTelemetry spans by splitting the route file's absolute path on the first `/app/` segment, so an extra `app` segment degrades transaction names, span segment names and `event.transaction` to `transaction_info.source: "url"`; with the root renamed (e.g. `nextapp`) they read `GET /api/users/[id]` with `source: "route"`.

Where the docs stand: ~/dev/sentry-docs/docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nextjs/index.mdx:4 (`sdk: sentry.javascript.nextjs`); nextjs/index.mdx:53 (`sentry.edge.config.ts` — "Runs in edge runtimes"; only Client/Server/Edge named); nextjs/index.mdx:144-147 (`instrumentation.ts` branches only on `NEXT_RUNTIME === "nodejs"` and `"edge"`); ~/dev/sentry-docs/docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx (two entries, index.mdx + configuration/, never mentions Next.js).

What ran: Next.js 15.5.4 (app router) with @sentry/nextjs 10.67.0 (develop, v11 line) on bun 1.4.0, run as `bun --bun next build` / `bun --bun next start`. Cell record.

Outgoing `fetch` is not instrumented under Bun, because `@sentry/node`'s HTTP integration listens on undici's `undici:request:*` diagnostics channels and Bun's native fetch does not publish them — install `@sentry/bun` and add its `fetchIntegration()` to `sentry.server.config.mjs`, guarded on `typeof globalThis.Bun !== 'undefined'` so Node deployments of the same build do not double-span. DOC-F16Remix on Bun: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a Remix v2 on Bun page for the Bun.build server bundle

Description

No page joins Remix and Bun. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/remix/index.mdx:12-16 carries the Remix 3 alert as the only runtime or version caveat, remix/index.mdx:58-97 covers only withSentry in root.tsx with no server entry, no instrumentation file and no run command, and remix/manual-setup.mdx:64 shows the Node install.

Verified on Remix v2 (@remix-run/dev Vite plugin with the @remix-run/express adapter) and @sentry/remix 10.67.0 on Bun 1.4.0: instrument.mjs imported as the first line of server.mjs, export const handleError = Sentry.sentryHandleError in app/entry.server.tsx, sentryRemixVitePlugin() in vite.config.ts, the mysql2 pool at module scope, then bundle the server entry with Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./server.mjs'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin, keeping @sentry/* and express external, and run bun dist/server.js. Hold the adapter handler in a variable and wrap it in Sentry.withIsolationScope(() => Sentry.continueTrace({ sentryTrace, baggage }, () => remixHandler(req, res, next))), or add bunHttpServerIntegration({ spans: false }) from @sentry/bun to Sentry.init.

Works today:

  • Unhandled loader errors via the handleError hook
  • Route names GET users/:id with source route, plus LOADER routes/users.$id children
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation, with the wrapper above
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 database spans
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • Plain bun server.mjs sends errors and logs only, with no transactions or spans at all.
  • The app's package.json must not carry "sideEffects": false, or the bundler drops side-effect-only imports and their libraries go uninstrumented.
  • The bundle is emitted without sourcemaps unless you enable them, so events lose context lines.

What to change:

Add a Koa on Deno page covering error capture and instrumentation limits

For automatic Koa error capture, bundle the server with esbuild and sentryOrchestrionPlugin() from @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild (format: 'esm', external: ['@sentry/deno', '@sentry/server-utils'], plus a createRequire banner) and run the bundle; without that build step, register app.on('error', err => Sentry.captureException(err)) yourself. Works today:

  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation.
  • Database spans for mysql, with the deferred hook registration.
  • Logs, carrying the request trace id.

Known limits:

  • Parameterized route names are not available: transactions are GET /users/42 with source url, and the streamed server span is GET with no http.route.
  • import "@sentry/deno/import" must not be the first static import: Koa's statuses dependency does require('*.json'), and Deno compiles JSON as JavaScript while any module load hook is registered (denoland/deno#36240), so the process dies at import.
  • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented; node:http requests are.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/frameworks/koa.mdx with the setup, both error-capture options and the limits.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F22, cell M-koa-deno

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/koa.mdx (new), with an "Instrumentation timing on Deno" section covering `--import` vs static import and the JSON load-hook workaround

A Koa-on-Deno page must exist and must document instrumentation timing, which is where every Koa capability succeeded or failed. It must say: (1) A static `import "@sentry/deno/import"` as the first import of the entry file does not work for Koa — Deno instantiates the whole module graph, including `koa`, before any module body executes, so the hook is registered too late; load it with the CLI flag instead (`deno run --import=./preload.ts main.ts`). (2) On Deno 2.9.0, registering any `Module.registerHooks` load hook breaks CJS `require('*.json')` (denoland/deno#36240), and Koa's `statuses` dependency does exactly that, so the app must also register a JSON-restoring load hook (`registerHooks({ load(url, ctx, next) { const r = next(url, ctx); return url.endsWith('.json') ? { ...r, format: 'json' } : r; } })` or the `readFileSync` equivalent); with the flag plus that hook, route names work. (3) An alternative that avoids the runtime hook entirely and was verified for both error capture and library spans: bundle the Koa app with esbuild and `sentryOrchestrionPlugin()` from `@sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild` (`format: 'esm'`, `external: ['@sentry/deno', '@sentry/server-utils']`, plus a `createRequire` banner) and run the bundle with Deno — no import hook and no manual `app.on('error')` handler needed. (4) For database spans without bundling, keep `import "@sentry/deno/import"` out of the static import list and register it in the entry body after Koa is imported (`await import('@sentry/deno/import')`), then dynamically import the driver. Isolation, incoming trace continuation and logs need no caveat. Outgoing `fetch` is not instrumented — no `http.client` span, no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` — in both `static` and `stream` lifecycles; the page must ship the userland fetch integration built from `addFetchInstrumentationHandler` / `defineIntegration` / `getClient` / `instrumentFetchRequest`, and note `node:http`/`node:https` are traced normally.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/koa/index.mdx:4 `sdk: sentry.javascript.node`, :5 `fallbackGuide: javascript.node`, :12 body is only `<PlatformContent includePath="getting-started-node" />`; `grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/koa/` returns nothing; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-232 never mentions Koa or any framework, and no page in the repo mentions `@sentry/deno/import` or `Module.registerHooks`

What ran: Koa 3.2.1 + @koa/router 15.7.0 on Deno 2.9.0 (aarch64-apple-darwin), @sentry/deno 10.67.0 from develop (worktree tarball). Cell record.

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/oak.mdx (new), with the `Deno.serve(app.fetch)` vs `app.listen()` warning as its first section

An Oak page must exist and must lead with the mounting shape, because the two Oak mounting styles are not equivalent for Sentry: mount with `Deno.serve(app.fetch)` and never with `app.listen()` — under `app.listen()` handlers run outside the request's async context, so there is no active span and errors are never captured at all. With `Deno.serve(app.fetch)`, errors, isolation, incoming trace continuation, database spans and logs all work with no extra wiring. Two caveats must be documented. (1) Route names: Oak spans are named by HTTP method only until the app names them; register a `router.use()` middleware BEFORE any route on that router that reads `ctx.matched?.at(-1)?.path` and calls `Sentry.updateSpanName(Sentry.getActiveSpan(), \`${ctx.request.method} ${route}\`)`. (2) Outgoing requests: `@sentry/deno` does not instrument Deno's global `fetch` — no `http.client` span, no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` header, so the downstream service starts a new trace. Two fixes were verified in both trace lifecycles: use `node:http` for the outgoing call (zero code beyond the client swap — `http.get(url, ...)` produced `http.client GET <url>` plus both headers), or keep `fetch` and register a userland fetch integration built from `@sentry/core` primitives. The page must also show the `deno.json` shape the run needed: `{"nodeModulesDir": "manual", "imports": {"@oak/oak": "jsr:@oak/oak@^17", "@sentry/deno": "npm:@sentry/deno"}}` with the SDK present in a real `node_modules`.

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/fresh.mdx (new, following the guides/cloudflare/frameworks/*.mdx pattern), plus a new guides/deno/frameworks/index.mdx listing the supported Deno frameworks

A Fresh 2 page must exist and state that Fresh on Deno is viable but that nothing framework-specific is instrumented automatically — a hand-written middleware is required. It must document, in this order: (1) Setup — scaffold with `deno run -A jsr:@fresh/init@^2`, set `"nodeModulesDir": "manual"` in `deno.json`, keep `import "@sentry/deno/import"` as the first line of `instrument.ts` and `instrument.ts` as the first import of `main.ts`. (2) Errors — Fresh catches route errors itself and returns its own 500 page, so unhandled errors never reach Sentry on their own; the verified fix is `integrations: [Sentry.captureConsoleIntegration({ levels: ['error'], handled: false })]` in `Sentry.init`, which reports them as real exceptions carrying the request's trace id, in both `deno task dev` and the `deno serve _fresh/server.js` build. (3) Route names — spans stay unparameterized unless the app renames them: register a middleware right after `staticFiles()` that reads `ctx.route` and calls `Sentry.updateSpanName` on the root span, which is the only way to get `sentry.segment.name.source: 'route'`. The page must also explain why Fresh's own route enrichment has no effect: the Deno SDK registers an OpenTelemetry TracerProvider but no ContextManager, so `trace.getActiveSpan()` from `@opentelemetry/api` returns `undefined` and the `http.route` Fresh sets there is dropped. (4) Isolation — per-request isolation is not reliable on Deno: Deno leaks the AsyncLocalStorage store out of a handler that awaits a dynamic `import()` (Node does not), so `Sentry.setTag`/`setUser`/`setContext`/`addBreadcrumb` from one request can attach to every later request; the app-level mitigation is to replace the isolation scope in the middleware before `ctx.next()` (new `Sentry.Scope`, `setClient(Sentry.getClient())`, carry over the propagation context and SDK processing metadata). (5) Outgoing fetch — no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers for native `fetch`; ship the userland `fetchTracingIntegration` built from `addFetchInstrumentationHandler`, `defineIntegration` and `instrumentFetchRequest`. (6) Database spans — `@fresh/plugin-vite` sets `resolve.noExternal: true`, so every instrumented driver must be listed in BOTH `ssr: { external: [...] }` (for `deno task dev`) and `environments: { ssr: { build: { rollupOptions: { external: [...] } } } }` (for `deno task build`); `ssr.external` alone does not survive the build. Incoming trace continuation and logs need no caveat.

Where the docs stand: No page mentions Fresh: `grep -rniE "\bfresh\b" docs/platforms/javascript/` returns only docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/features/agents-sdk.mdx:39 ("a fresh conversation ID"), and there is no docs/platforms/javascript/guides/fresh/ directory; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-232 is the entire Deno guide (plus configuration/integrations/{breadcrumbs,contextlines}.mdx) and names no framework — its only wiring is `import * as Sentry from "@sentry/deno"` + `Sentry.init` at :43-88, `--allow-net` at :106 and `--allow-read` at :125

Verified on Nuxt 4 with @sentry/nuxt 10.67.0 and Deno 2.9.0: do not set a Nitro preset. Build the default node-server output with npx nuxt build, keep sentry: { autoInjectServerSentry: 'top-level-import' } in nuxt.config.ts and a root sentry.server.config.ts that calls Sentry.init(), then run the output under Deno with cd .output/server && PORT=<port> deno run --allow-net --allow-read --allow-write --allow-env --allow-sys --allow-ffi --unstable-byonm ./index.mjs. Nitro's node-server entry uses node:http, which the SDK instruments on Deno. For outgoing requests, add winterCGFetchIntegration() from @sentry/vercel-edge to Sentry.init.

Works today:

  • Errors from nitro route handlers, with request data and source context.
  • Parameterized GET /api/users/:id with source route.
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation.
  • http.client spans and sentry-trace/baggage on fetch and nitro's $fetch, with the integration above.
  • Logs.

Known limits:

  • With nitro: { preset: 'deno_server' } there is no request instrumentation at all: no http.server spans, getActiveSpan() is undefined in handlers, every request shares one isolation scope and one trace id so tags and users leak, and incoming trace headers are ignored.
  • Outgoing fetch is not instrumented on Deno without the integration above.

What to change:

  • guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:7: qualify the "work out of the box" claim; add a Deno section next to Cloudflare Pages at :9 with the run command and the deno_server warning.
  • Cross-link a Deno framework page for Nuxt.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F28, cell M-nuxt-deno

The presets page must stop implying that every non-Cloudflare preset works out of the box and must add a Deno section saying the opposite for `deno_server`: with `nitro: { preset: 'deno_server' }` there is no request instrumentation — route names are not parameterized, every request shares one isolation scope and one trace id (so tags, users and breadcrumbs leak between requests), and incoming `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers are not continued. The verified guidance is: do not set a Nitro preset on Deno. Keep the default `node-server` preset (`npx nuxt build`) and run the produced Node output under Deno with `cd .output/server && PORT=<port> deno run --allow-net --allow-read --allow-write --allow-env --allow-sys --allow-ffi --unstable-byonm ./index.mjs`, with `sentry: { autoInjectServerSentry: 'top-level-import' }`; that restores route names, incoming trace continuation, per-request isolation and route-level errors with request data. The page must also cover the two remaining Deno gaps: (a) outgoing requests made with `fetch` or nitro's `$fetch` get no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers because Deno's fetch is not backed by `node:http`/undici — add `winterCGFetchIntegration()` from `@sentry/vercel-edge` in `sentry.server.config.ts` (and `traceLifecycle: 'stream'` in the verified configuration); (b) if `deno_server` is used anyway, database drivers must stay bundled — the verified config is `nitro: { preset: 'deno_server', externals: { inline: ['generate-function', 'named-placeholders'], external: ['mysql'] } }`, because externalizing a driver routes it through Deno's module hook where `require()` of a JSON file fails. Errors and logs work in all cases.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:7 claims "The Sentry Nuxt SDK provides support for different Nitro deployment presets, most of which **work out of the box**" and the 11-line file then documents only Cloudflare Pages (:9-11) — the run contradicts this for the `deno_server` preset, which produced no request instrumentation at all; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/install/cli-import.mdx:33 prescribes `node --import ./.output/server/sentry.server.config.mjs .output/server/index.mjs`, a Node-only start command; `grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/` returns 0 hits and `grep -rniE "nuxt|nitro" docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/` returns 0 hits

Known limits:

  • Without fetchIntegration(), neither a global fetch() nor SvelteKit's event.fetch produces an http.client span or carries trace headers on Deno.

  • Fix manual-setup__v10.7.0.mdx:21-35 to name Deno with adapter-node as supported.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F29, cell M-sveltekit-deno

Page: guides/sveltekit/manual-setup__v10.7.0.mdx:21-35 — correct the "Currently not supported" bullet and add Deno/adapter-node; restore an adapter-and-runtime compatibility section in guides/sveltekit/manual-setup.mdx (which currently has none), and add guides/deno/frameworks/sveltekit.mdx with the run command, the `fetchIntegration` snippet and the mysql2 `createRequire` workaround

The adapter-compatibility note must be corrected to say that SvelteKit runs on Deno through adapter-node with no source changes: build with `vite build` under Node, then start the adapter output with `HOST=… PORT=… deno run --no-prompt --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read --allow-sys --allow-ffi build/index.js`. Errors, route names, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation and logs all work unchanged. Two Deno-specific caveats must be added. (1) Outgoing HTTP: `@sentry/node`'s fetch instrumentation is inert on Deno because it listens on undici `diagnostics_channel` events and Deno's global `fetch` is native, so neither a global `fetch()` nor SvelteKit's `event.fetch` produces an `http.client` span or carries `sentry-trace`/`baggage`. The verified fix is to register `fetchIntegration` from `@sentry/cloudflare` (already installed as a dependency of `@sentry/sveltekit`, so no new install) in `src/hooks.server.js`, gated to Deno so Node builds are not double-instrumented. (2) Database spans: they work on Deno with adapter-node and no flags for drivers that do not read their own `package.json` at import time (`mysql` needs nothing). Deno has an open bug (denoland/deno#36240) where registering any module hook makes `require('*.json')` compile as JavaScript, which breaks drivers like `mysql2`; the verified workaround is to force the driver's `package.json` into the CJS cache before `Sentry.init` runs, i.e. `createRequire(import.meta.url)('mysql2/package.json')` at the top of `src/hooks.server.js` followed by a dynamic `await import('@sentry/sveltekit')`.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/sveltekit/manual-setup__v10.7.0.mdx:21-35 ("Notes on SvelteKit adapter compatibility") lists at :30-31 under "Currently not supported" — "Non-Node.js server runtimes, such as Vercel's edge runtime, are not yet supported" — which reads as excluding Deno, while :28 lists Adapter-node as fully supported; the run contradicts the exclusion (the adapter-node production build ran under Deno with errors, route names, isolation, incoming trace, db spans and logs all working). The current docs/platforms/javascript/guides/sveltekit/manual-setup.mdx has no such expandable at all (its body is only `sentry-init/manual-setup-callout` + `getting-started-complete`, :1-9), so the v11 line now makes no runtime statement whatsoever; `grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/sveltekit/` returns 0 hits

What ran: SvelteKit 2 (@sveltejs/kit 2.x, adapter-node 5.x, svelte 5, vite 6) + @sentry/sveltekit 10.67.0 (develop) on Deno 2.9.0 (aarch64-apple-darwin), running the adapter-node production build. Cell record.

(1) Build-time instrumentation is required instead of the runtime require hook: add the documented `sentryReactRouter` Vite plugin, and set `ssr: { noExternal: true, external: ['@sentry/react-router', '@sentry/node'] }` in `vite.config.ts` so the injected snippet's CommonJS chunk does not default-import ESM-only `@sentry/core`. Without this, database spans are missing, because Deno cannot compile the `require('./package.json')` calls drivers such as mysql2 make at load time. (2) Outgoing requests made with Deno's built-in global `fetch` are not traced — no `http.client` span is created and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers are attached — because Deno's `fetch` is native and does not emit the undici `diagnostics_channel` events the Node SDK subscribes to, so the trace ends at the server. The verified fix that needs no extra dependency is a userland integration passed to `Sentry.init`: `defineIntegration(() => ({ name: 'DenoFetch', setupOnce() { addFetchInstrumentationHandler(d => { const client = getClient(); if (!client || isSentryRequestUrl(d.fetchData.url, client)) return; instrumentFetchRequest(...); }); } }))` from public `@sentry/core` exports. Errors, route names, isolation, incoming trace continuation and logs all work.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/react-router/ contains only index.mdx, manual-setup.mdx and profiling/, and `grep -rni deno` over it returns nothing; `grep -rniE 'react.?router' docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/` also returns nothing. A page must state that Fastify does not run on Cloudflare Workers behind httpServerHandler, and why: constructing the app at module scope throws a disallowed-global-scope error because Fastify's boot generates random values and schedules work, and lazy construction on first request then hits a second workerd restriction that blocks serving. No Sentry capability could be exercised. The statement must be scoped to Workers; Fastify on Node is unaffected.

DOC-F40Hapi on Cloudflare: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a Hapi on Cloudflare Workers page and document build-time instrumentation

Description

Nothing documents Hapi on Workers. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hapi/index.mdx:4-5 sets sdk: sentry.javascript.node with fallbackGuide: javascript.node and its whole body is one include at :12. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/ holds no hapi.mdx and the framework list at guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 omits Hapi. Separately, sentryOrchestrionPlugin and @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion appear nowhere in the docs, although packages/server-utils/package.json:30-62 exports six orchestrion entry points.

Verified on the develop line: @hapi/hapi 21 on workerd with compatibility_date: "2026-05-01" and compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat", "enable_nodejs_http_server_modules"], served through httpServerHandler from cloudflare:node and wrapped with Sentry.withSentry() from @sentry/cloudflare. Build-time instrumentation is what makes the framework visible, by either of two public routes: sentryCloudflareVitePlugin() from @sentry/cloudflare/vite in a Vite build, or sentryOrchestrionPlugin() from @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild when you pre-bundle the Worker yourself and point wrangler's main at the output.

Works today:

  • Parameterized transaction names (GET /users/{id}, source route) with nested router and extension spans, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers, db spans from a mysql query, Sentry.logger logs.

Known limits:

  • Errors thrown in a Hapi route handler are not captured on Workers: hapi's request/error event never fires there, so nothing reaches Sentry.
  • A plain wrangler build gives neither route names nor database spans; wrangler's own bundler takes no plugins.
  • mysql2 cannot be used on workerd; mysql 2.x works.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/hapi.mdx with the wiring above and link it from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23.
  • Add guides/cloudflare/features/build-time-instrumentation.mdx covering sentryOrchestrionPlugin for esbuild, rollup and webpack, and link it from the Vite plugin page.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F40, cell M-hapi-cf

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/hapi.mdx (new), linked from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23, plus a new shared page guides/cloudflare/features/build-time-instrumentation.mdx covering sentryOrchestrionPlugin for non-Vite bundlers, which is exported but documented nowhere.

A page must say that Hapi 21 runs on Workers behind the node:http shim, and document one hard defect plus two build shapes. On workerd the response emits close before finish with writableEnded still false, hapi reads that as a client disconnect, turns every response into a 499, and its request and error events never fire, so the Hapi integration captures nothing. Show the app-side fix, an onRequest extension that drops the premature close before hapi's transmit sees it, which restores capture, the transaction name and the auto.function.hapi mechanism. Parameterized transaction names require build-time instrumentation, either the Cloudflare Vite plugin or the orchestrion plugin for esbuild, rollup or webpack, with a plain-wrangler fallback that renames the root span in an onPreResponse extension. Database spans need the same build-time instrumentation. Isolation, incoming trace, outgoing fetch and logs work unaided.

Where the docs stand: guides/hapi/index.mdx:4-5 and :12 (Node fallback, body is one include); guides/cloudflare/frameworks/ has no hapi.mdx; guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 (no Hapi); no mention of sentryOrchestrionPlugin or @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion anywhere in docs, although packages/server-utils/package.json:30-54 exports six orchestrion entry points.

DOC-F50Remix on Cloudflare: documented wrongP0proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Rewrite the Remix on Cloudflare page for Workers and fix the Vite plugin options

Description

docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/remix.mdx is 60 lines and does not describe a working Workers setup: :39-49 installs only @sentry/remix with no @sentry/cloudflare, and :55-58 embeds the shared include. In that include, the Cloudflare branch at platform-includes/getting-started-complete/javascript.remix.mdx:263-313 covers Cloudflare Pages only, and the "Capture Server-Side Errors" step at :315-347 sits outside any PlatformSection and tells every reader to import from bare @sentry/remix at :333. Separately, guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx:30-47 presents _experimental.useDiagnosticsChannelInjection as required.

Verified on the develop line: Remix v2 (2.17.5) with the Remix Vite plugin on workerd, compatibility_date: "2026-05-01", compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat"]. Install @sentry/remix and @sentry/cloudflare. In app/entry.server.tsx, export const handleError = sentryHandleError imported from @sentry/remix/cloudflare. In the worker entry, Sentry.withSentry() from @sentry/cloudflare around a handler built with createRequestHandler(instrumentBuild(remixBuild)). In vite.config.ts, sentryCloudflareVitePlugin() from @sentry/cloudflare/vite at its defaults alongside the Remix plugin, with ssr: { target: 'webworker', noExternal: true }.

Works today:

  • Loader, action and resource-route errors, parameterized route names with http.route, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers, db spans from mysql, Sentry.logger logs.

Known limits:

  • Importing from bare @sentry/remix in a webworker SSR build leaves handleError undefined and captures nothing.
  • A route with no loader or action stays method-only.
  • Without sentryCloudflareVitePlugin() there are no database spans and no warning; mysql2 is unusable on workerd.

What to change:

  • Rewrite guides/cloudflare/frameworks/remix.mdx: add @sentry/cloudflare to the install at :39-49, add a Workers entry section, point handleError at @sentry/remix/cloudflare, and add a database-spans section.
  • Correct guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx:30-47 to the shipped buildTimeInstrumentation and autoInstrumentation options, both defaulting to true.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F50, cell M-remix-cf

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/remix.mdx — rewrite to cover Workers: add `@sentry/cloudflare` to the install at :39-49, add a Workers entry section (`withSentry` + `instrumentBuild`), override the include's `@sentry/remix` import with `@sentry/remix/cloudflare`, and add a database-spans section (`sentryCloudflareVitePlugin()` plus `ssr.noExternal`); additionally correct docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx:30-47 where `useDiagnosticsChannelInjection` is presented as required

The page contradicts the run on three points and must be rewritten for Workers. (1) Error capture: on Cloudflare Workers and Pages the `handleError` import must come from `@sentry/remix/cloudflare`, never bare `@sentry/remix` — the bare specifier silently captures nothing in a `webworker` SSR build. Both `export const handleError = sentryHandleError` and `wrapHandleErrorWithSentry` work from that subpath, on both trace lifecycles, for loaders, actions, resource routes and thrown 5xx Responses. (2) Request isolation: the worker entry must be wrapped with `withSentry` from `@sentry/cloudflare` around a handler built with `instrumentBuild` from `@sentry/remix/cloudflare`. `wrapRequestHandler` from `@sentry/cloudflare/request` exists only for runtimes that cannot enable `nodejs_compat` (Shopify Oxygen); on Workers it does not establish isolation unless `setAsyncLocalStorageAsyncContextStrategy` is called first. (3) Database spans require the build-time transform: add `sentryCloudflareVitePlugin()` to the Vite config alongside the Remix plugin — defaults are sufficient, `autoInstrumentation: false` is unnecessary, and the connection may sit at module scope or inside the loader — and set `ssr: { target: 'webworker', noExternal: true }`. Without the plugin, queries run normally, no `db` span appears, and the SDK logs no warning. The `_experimental.useDiagnosticsChannelInjection: true` requirement stated on the Vite plugin page is no longer needed on the v11 line and must be corrected there too. Finally, the page must also install `@sentry/cloudflare`, not just `@sentry/remix`.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/remix.mdx is 60 lines and is wrong for Workers: :39-49 installs only `@sentry/remix` (no `@sentry/cloudflare`), and :55-58 embeds the shared include. In that include, the Cloudflare branch platform-includes/getting-started-complete/javascript.remix.mdx:263-313 covers Cloudflare Pages only — a `functions/_middleware.js` using `Sentry.sentryPagesPlugin` (:274-307) — with no Workers entry at all. The "Capture Server-Side Errors" step at :315-347 is outside any PlatformSection and tells every reader, Cloudflare included, to `import * as Sentry from "@sentry/remix"` (:333) and assign `Sentry.wrapHandleErrorWithSentry` / `Sentry.sentryHandleError` (:335-342). The run contradicts this: the bare specifier resolves to the browser build in the Remix Vite `webworker` SSR build, leaving `handleError` undefined and capturing nothing, with only a rollup warning. `instrumentBuild` and `@sentry/remix/cloudflare` appear in the docs only on the Oxygen page, docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/hydrogen-remix.mdx:32-42 and :91. `grep -rn noExternal` over docs and platform-includes returns zero hits. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx:30-47 tells users to enable `_experimental.useDiagnosticsChannelInjection: true`, which the run shows is not needed.

What ran: Remix v2 (2.17.5, Remix Vite plugin, resource routes) on Cloudflare Workers (wrangler 4.129.0 dev / workerd, compatibility_date 2026-05-01, nodejs_compat). Cell record.

DOC-R1Stream-mode includes call `'static'` the default; develop defaults to `'stream'`P0verified in source

Proposed issue docs-change

Fix inverted traceLifecycle default in the stream mode includes

Description

The three stream-mode includes present stream mode as opt-in. platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.bun.mdx:5 (and javascript.deno.mdx:5, javascript.cloudflare.mdx:8) say "Opt in by setting the traceLifecycle option to 'stream'", and each closes with "set traceLifecycle to 'static' (the default)" (javascript.bun.mdx:25, javascript.deno.mdx:24, javascript.cloudflare.mdx:62).

On develop the default is 'stream'. packages/core/src/client.ts:242 normalizes traceLifecycle: options.traceLifecycle === 'static' ? 'static' : 'stream', so every value except an explicit 'static' resolves to stream. No runtime overrides this, and packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-serve.ts:28 describes 'stream' as the default too.

What to change

  • Reframe all three includes as opt-out: 'stream' is the default, 'static' is what a user sets.
  • Replace the traceLifecycle: "stream" sample lines with "static" examples, or drop them.
  • Add the missing comma after tracesSampleRate: 0.2 at javascript.cloudflare.mdx:19.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-R1

Status quo. platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.bun.mdx:5, javascript.deno.mdx:5 and javascript.cloudflare.mdx:8 all open with "Opt in by setting the `traceLifecycle` option to `'stream'`". Each closes with "To revert to transaction mode, set `traceLifecycle` to `'static'` (the default)" (bun:25, deno:24, cloudflare:62).

On develop. packages/core/src/client.ts:242 normalizes `traceLifecycle: options.traceLifecycle === 'static' ? 'static' : 'stream'`, so every value other than an explicit `'static'` resolves to stream. No runtime overrides it, and packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-serve.ts:28 also calls `'stream'` the default.

Work item. Reframe all three includes as opt-out: stream is the default and `'static'` is the option a user sets. Also fix the missing comma after `tracesSampleRate: 0.2` at javascript.cloudflare.mdx:19, which makes that snippet a SyntaxError.

DOC-R2Deno crons page recommends denoCronIntegration(), which throws on Deno 2.9P0verified in source

Proposed issue docs-change

Stop recommending denoCronIntegration() on the Deno crons page

Description

platform-includes/crons/setup/javascript.deno.mdx:1 heads its first section "Automatic Check-Ins (Recommended)" and :12 tells the reader to pass Sentry.denoCronIntegration() to Sentry.init.

That call crashes startup on current Deno. packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-cron.ts:23 assigns Deno.cron = new Proxy(Deno.cron, ...), but on Deno 2.9.0 Deno.cron is a getter-only property, so Sentry.init throws TypeError: Cannot set property cron of #<Object> which has only a getter. This reproduced in four separate audit runs. Line 18 returns silently when Deno.cron is missing, so without --unstable-cron the integration is a no-op. It is also not in the default set at packages/deno/src/sdk.ts:31-49, contrary to its JSDoc at deno-cron.ts:64.

What to change

  • Remove the "Recommended" framing and the denoCronIntegration() snippet, or label the integration as broken on Deno 2.9.
  • Lead with the Sentry.withMonitor / Sentry.captureCheckIn sections already on the page.
  • State that Deno.cron needs --unstable-cron.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-R2

Status quo. platform-includes/crons/setup/javascript.deno.mdx:1 heads the section "Automatic Check-Ins (Recommended)" and :12 shows `integrations: [Sentry.denoCronIntegration()]` as the way to monitor `Deno.cron` calls.

On develop. packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-cron.ts:23 assigns `Deno.cron = new Proxy(Deno.cron, ...)`. On Deno 2.9.0 `Deno.cron` is a getter-only property, so `Sentry.init` with this integration throws `TypeError: Cannot set property cron of #<Object> which has only a getter` (reproduced in four separate audit runs). Line 18 returns silently when `Deno.cron` is absent, so without `--unstable-cron` the integration does nothing. It is also absent from the default set at packages/deno/src/sdk.ts:31-49, contradicting its own JSDoc at deno-cron.ts:64.

Work item. Drop the "Recommended" framing and the `denoCronIntegration()` snippet, or mark the integration as currently broken on Deno 2.9. Lead the page with the `Sentry.withMonitor` / `Sentry.captureCheckIn` path that the other sections already document, and state the `--unstable-cron` requirement.

That flag is computed at :113-120 from the engine-supplied step context: with `attempt` and `config.retries.limit` present, only `attempt > retryLimit` reports, so intermediate retries send nothing; without a step context every attempt is captured.

Work item. Add an error-capture section stating that a `step.do()` failure is reported once, on the final attempt, when the Workflows engine supplies a step context, and that on engines without one every attempt reports.
